| 2026-10-10 | claude | M2.4.1 | Built dual-screen mode (spec 5.3.3): off-by-default `projects.dual_screen_enabled` (migration `20261010_0039`, up/down/up + `alembic check` clean, single head) + owner-only `GET/PUT .../screening/dual-screen`. New `app/screening.py::human_decisions_by_decider`/`dual_screen_status`/`visible_rows`/`has_any_human_decision` replace "last human row wins" with per-decider tracking (`unscreened`/`awaiting_second`/`agreed`/`conflict`) when the mode is on; only `agreed` counts as decided. Blinding: the queue keeps a source for a reviewer until *they* personally decide it (even if someone else already has) and withholds the AI suggestion meanwhile; `GET .../decisions` hides another decider's row for a source+stage until the caller has their own; undo only takes back your own decision. Fixed two downstream consumers that would otherwise misread a conflict under the old "last row wins" rule: `thematic_clusters.py::clusterable_sources` (and `coverage_matrix.py`, which reuses it) now require `agreed` to treat a source as excluded when dual-screen is on. Single-reviewer mode (default) is untouched — gated on `project.dual_screen_enabled` throughout, proven by the full pre-existing `test_screening.py`/`test_role_matrix.py` suites passing unchanged. New tests: 7 in `test_screening.py`, 2 `test_role_matrix.py` rows. Focused run: 524 passed.
